2. Die \_\_\_\_\_\_ was so dik dat ons nie die pad kon sien nie. mes / **mis ✓** / mus / mos
3. In die grot het ons ons eie \_\_\_\_\_\_ gehoor. ego / ekko / **eggo ✓** / eg
4. As jy \_\_\_\_\_\_, bly waar jy is. verdwyn / verdraai / verdeel / **verdwaal ✓**
5. Die arend bou sy nes teen die hoë \_\_\_\_\_\_. kraan / **krans ✓** / kruis / kras

## Level 4 – Afrikaans 8: Die vleiland

Topic: Nature and outdoors. About 253 words, 22 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**vleiland** – 'n Nat, moerasagtige gebied waar water vlak bo of in die grond lê. Bv. Baie watervoëls woon in die vleiland. Een vleiland, baie vleilande. Engels: "wetland".

**riet** – 'n Hoë, dun plant wat in water of nat grond groei. Bv. Die voël het sy nes tussen die riete gebou. Een riet, baie riete. Engels: "reed".

**spons** – Iets sags met baie gaatjies wat water opsuig. Bv. Ma was die skottelgoed met 'n spons. Een spons, baie sponse. Engels: "sponge".

**oorstroom** – Wanneer water oor die walle loop en die land bedek. Bv. Na die swaar reën het die rivier oorstroom. Dit oorstroom, dit het oorstroom. Engels: "flood".

**filtreer** – Om vuilheid uit iets te haal deur dit deur fyn gaatjies te laat gaan. Bv. Die masjien filtreer die swembadwater. Ek filtreer, ek het gefiltreer. Engels: "filter".

**vernietig** – Om iets so te breek of te beskadig dat dit nie meer bestaan nie. Bv. Die storm het die ou skuur vernietig. Ek vernietig, ek het vernietig. Engels: "destroy".

*Extra cards for more support:* biesies, plakkaat

### Die leesstuk

Anke se klas gaan op 'n uitstappie na 'n vleiland naby hul dorp. Sy het verwag dat dit net 'n modderige, vervelige plek gaan wees. Maar sodra hulle op die houtbrug stap, verander sy van plan.

Oral om hulle groei lang riete en biesies wat saggies in die wind wieg. Honderde voëls roep, fluit en plas in die vlak water. Die gids wys vir hulle 'n grys reier en 'n klein, bont ysvoël. Uit die riete kom die harde, snaakse geluid van paddas.

"'n Vleiland is soos 'n reuse-spons," verduidelik die gids. "Wanneer dit baie reën, suig dit die water op en keer dat die dorp oorstroom. In droë tye laat dit die water stadig los, sodat die riviere nie opdroog nie." Die plante in die vleiland maak ook die water skoon deur vuilheid uit te filtreer.

Ongelukkig word baie vleilande in Suid-Afrika vernietig. Mense bou huise en paaie daarop, of hulle gooi rommel in die water. Wanneer 'n vleiland verdwyn, verloor die voëls, paddas en visse hul huis. Die dorp kry ook meer oorstromings en vuiler water.

Voor hulle huis toe gaan, help die klas om plastiek uit die riete te haal. Anke vind 'n ou koeldrankbottel en drie plastieksakke. Op die bus terug sit sy stil en dink. Sy het nie geweet dat 'n modderige plek so belangrik kan wees nie. Toe hulle by die skool aankom, vra sy vir haar juffrou of die klas 'n plakkaat oor die beskerming van vleilande kan maak. Juffrou knik en glimlag. "Dit is presies waarom ons die uitstappie gedoen het," sê sy.

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Wat het Anke van die vleiland verwag?** 'n Mooi park. / **'n Modderige, vervelige plek. ✓** / 'n Groot meer. / 'n Dieretuin. / 'n Strand.
2. **Waarmee vergelyk die gids 'n vleiland?** Met 'n emmer. / Met 'n dam. / **Met 'n reuse-spons. ✓** / Met 'n swembad. / Met 'n rivier.
3. **Hoe maak die plante in die vleiland die water skoon?** Hulle drink die vuil water. / Hulle maak die water warm. / Hulle verf die water. / **Hulle filtreer die vuilheid uit. ✓** / Hulle laat die water verdamp.
4. **Wat gebeur met die dorp as 'n vleiland verdwyn?** Dit word groter. / Dit kry meer voëls. / Dit word droër. / Niks verander nie. / **Dit kry meer oorstromings en vuiler water. ✓**
5. **(Dinkvraag) Hoe het Anke se mening oor vleilande verander?** **Sy dink nou vleilande is baie belangrik en wil help om hulle te beskerm. ✓** / Sy dink dit is nog steeds vervelig. / Sy is bang vir paddas. / Sy wil nooit weer gaan nie. / Sy wil 'n huis daar bou.

### Woordherkenning (10)

vleiland, modderige, vervelige, riete, reier, ysvoël, oorstroom, filtreer, vernietig, plakkaat

### Grammatika: samestellings (5)

*Maak een woord van die twee – Bv. son + blom = sonblom*

1. vlei + land – **vleiland**
2. hout + brug – **houtbrug**
3. water + voël – **watervoël**
4. plastiek + sak – **plastieksak**
5. koeldrank + bottel – **koeldrankbottel**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. Baie watervoëls woon in die \_\_\_\_\_\_. vlakte / **vleiland ✓** / vleis / vlieënier
2. Die voël het sy nes tussen die hoë \_\_\_\_\_\_ gebou. **riete ✓** / rite / ruite / reste
3. Ma was die bord met 'n \_\_\_\_\_\_. sponse / **spons ✓** / spook / sponde
4. Na die swaar reën het die rivier \_\_\_\_\_\_. oorstroming / oorsteek / **oorstroom ✓** / oorweeg
5. Die storm het die ou skuur \_\_\_\_\_\_. vernietiging / verniet / vernuwe / **vernietig ✓**

## Level 4 – Afrikaans 9: Die aasvoëlrestaurant

Topic: Animals. About 239 words, 20 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**aasvoël** – 'n Groot voël wat die vleis van dooie diere eet. Bv. Die aasvoëls sirkel hoog in die lug. Een aasvoël, baie aasvoëls. Engels: "vulture".

**karkas** – Die liggaam van 'n dooie dier. Bv. Die hiënas eet aan die karkas. Een karkas, baie karkasse. Engels: "carcass".

**verteer** – Wanneer jou maag kos opbreek sodat jou liggaam dit kan gebruik. Bv. Dit neem ure om 'n groot maaltyd te verteer. Ek verteer, ek het verteer. Engels: "digest".

**versprei** – Om na baie plekke uit te brei. Bv. Kieme versprei maklik as jy nie jou hande was nie. Dit versprei, dit het versprei. Engels: "spread".

**verkyker** – 'n Instrument met twee lense waardeur jy dinge ver weg groter sien. Bv. Ons kyk deur die verkyker na die voëls. Een verkyker, baie verkykers. Engels: "binoculars".

**neerdaal** – Om van hoog af stadig na onder te kom. Bv. Die vliegtuig daal op die lughawe neer. Ek daal neer, ek het neergedaal. Engels: "descend".

*Extra cards for more support:* vlerkspan, grillerig

### Die leesstuk

Op 'n plaas naby die Magaliesberg sien Dawid vir die eerste keer 'n "restaurant" vir voëls. Dit is 'n oop stuk veld waar boere dooie diere neersit sodat aasvoëls dit kan eet. Dit klink dalk grillerig, maar dit help om hierdie belangrike voëls te red.

Aasvoëls is die natuur se skoonmaakspan. Hulle eet die karkasse van dooie diere wat andersins sou vrot en siektes versprei. Hul maag is so sterk dat hulle vleis kan verteer wat vir ander diere giftig sou wees. 'n Groep aasvoëls kan 'n hele karkas binne 'n paar uur skoon eet.

Ongelukkig is baie aasvoëls in Suid-Afrika in gevaar. Party vlieg teen kragdrade vas, en ander eet vleis wat vergiftig is. Party mense vang hulle ook onwettig.

By die restaurant kry die aasvoëls vleis wat veilig en skoon is. Dawid kyk deur 'n verkyker terwyl tientalle Kaapse aasvoëls uit die lug neerdaal. Hul vlerke is so breed dat een aasvoël se vlerkspan langer as 'n bed is. Hulle stoei en skree oor die kos, en die stof trek hoog in die lug.

Die boer vertel dat daar twintig jaar gelede net 'n paar aasvoëls in die omgewing was. Nou kom daar weer honderde. "Elke keer wat ek hulle sien, weet ek ons help die natuur," sê hy trots. Dawid besluit om 'n skoolprojek oor aasvoëls te doen. Hy wil hê meer mense moet weet dat selfs voëls wat lelik lyk, baie belangrik is. Sy titel? "Die natuur se helde met die kaal koppe."

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Wat is 'n "aasvoëlrestaurant"?** 'n Restaurant waar mense hoender eet. / **'n Oop stuk veld waar boere dooie diere vir aasvoëls neersit. ✓** / 'n Hok vir siek voëls. / 'n Winkel wat voëlkos verkoop. / 'n Dieretuin.
2. **Waarom word aasvoëls die natuur se skoonmaakspan genoem?** Hulle bou skoon neste. / Hulle bad elke dag. / **Hulle eet karkasse wat andersins sou vrot en siektes versprei. ✓** / Hulle eet rommel. / Hulle vee die veld skoon.
3. **Watter gevare bedreig aasvoëls?** Reën en koue. / Honde en katte. / Karre en treine. / **Kragdrade, vergiftigde vleis en onwettige vangs. ✓** / Ander voëls.
4. **Hoe breed is 'n Kaapse aasvoël se vlerke?** So breed soos 'n boek. / So breed soos 'n tafel. / So breed soos 'n huis. / So breed soos 'n hand. / **Die vlerkspan is langer as 'n bed. ✓**
5. **(Dinkvraag) Wat wil Dawid hê moet mense leer?** Dat aasvoëls gevaarlik is. / Dat aasvoëls mooi sing. / Dat hulle aasvoëls moet vang. / Dat aasvoëls vinnig vlieg. / **Dat selfs voëls wat lelik lyk, baie belangrik is. ✓**

### Woordherkenning (10)

aasvoëls, restaurant, grillerig, skoonmaakspan, karkasse, verteer, siektes, kragdrade, verkyker, vlerkspan

### Grammatika: teenoorgesteldes (5)

*Gee die teenoorgestelde – Bv. groot – klein*

1. lelik – **mooi**
2. lewend – **dood**
3. skoon – **vuil**
4. veilig – **gevaarlik** *(accept: onveilig)*
5. breed – **smal** *(accept: nou)*

### Woordeskat (5)

1. 'n Groep \_\_\_\_\_\_ sirkel hoog bo die veld. **aasvoëls ✓** / aasvoël / aas / aardvarke
2. Die hiënas eet aan die \_\_\_\_\_\_. kar / **karkas ✓** / karos / kraal
3. Dit neem ure om 'n groot maaltyd te \_\_\_\_\_\_. vertel / vertrek / **verteer ✓** / verteerbaar
4. Kieme \_\_\_\_\_\_ maklik as ons nie ons hande was nie. verspreiding / verspil / verskyn / **versprei ✓**
5. Ons kyk deur die \_\_\_\_\_\_ na die voëls. verkeer / **verkyker ✓** / verkoper / verkiesing

## Level 4 – Afrikaans 10: Sibu se skaakmat

Topic: Sport and games. About 255 words, 21 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**skaak** – 'n Bordspeletjie vir twee spelers met stukke soos 'n koning, 'n koningin en perde. Bv. Ons speel skaak op die stoep. Engels: "chess".

**teenstander** – Die persoon teen wie jy in 'n wedstryd of speletjie speel. Bv. My teenstander was baie sterk. Een teenstander, baie teenstanders. Engels: "opponent".

**skuif** – Om iets van een plek na 'n ander te beweeg; ook 'n beurt in 'n speletjie. Bv. Dit is jou skuif. Ek skuif, ek het geskuif. Engels: "move".

**toernooi** – 'n Kompetisie waarin baie spelers of spanne teen mekaar speel. Bv. Ons span het die tennistoernooi gewen. Een toernooi, baie toernooie. Engels: "tournament".

**kwartaal** – Een van die vier dele van die skooljaar. Bv. Die eerste kwartaal eindig in Maart. Een kwartaal, baie kwartale. Engels: "term".

**skaakmat** – Wanneer die koning vasgekeer is en nie kan ontsnap nie, sodat die speletjie verby is. Bv. Met een slim skuif het sy skaakmat gesê. Engels: "checkmate".

*Extra cards for more support:* beplan, finale

### Die leesstuk

Toe Sibu se oupa hom die eerste keer leer skaak speel het, het hy gedink dit is 'n vervelige speletjie. Daar was te veel reëls, en elke stuk het op 'n ander manier beweeg. Maar Oupa was geduldig, en stadig het Sibu begin verstaan.

Hy het geleer dat die koning die belangrikste stuk is, maar ook een van die swakste, want hy kan net een blokkie op 'n slag skuif. Die koningin kan in enige rigting so ver beweeg as wat sy wil. Die perd spring in die vorm van 'n L en kan oor ander stukke spring. "Skaak is soos 'n geveg sonder bloed," het Oupa gesê.

Elke middag na skool het hulle op die stoep gespeel. Aan die begin het Oupa elke keer gewen. Maar Sibu het goed opgelet hoe Oupa dink en beplan. Hy het geleer om nie net aan sy eie volgende skuif te dink nie, maar ook aan wat sy teenstander gaan doen.

Na 'n jaar het Sibu by die skool se skaakklub aangesluit. Aan die einde van die kwartaal was daar 'n toernooi teen ander skole. In die finale wedstryd het Sibu teen 'n ouer seun gespeel. Dit was so stil in die saal dat hy sy eie hart kon hoor klop. Na 'n uur het hy sy koningin versigtig vorentoe geskuif en gesê: "Skaakmat!"

Toe hy by die huis kom, het hy die medalje om Oupa se nek gehang. "Dit is eintlik joune," het hy gesê. Oupa het gelag en sy kop geskud. "Nee, jy het dit self gewen. Ek het jou net geleer om te dink."

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Wat het Sibu eers van skaak gedink?** Dat dit maklik is. / **Dat dit vervelig is. ✓** / Dat dit gevaarlik is. / Dat dit net vir meisies is. / Dat dit sy gunsteling is.
2. **Hoe beweeg die perd?** Een blokkie op 'n slag. / Net vorentoe. / **In die vorm van 'n L, en hy kan oor ander stukke spring. ✓** / Net skuins. / Hy kan nie beweeg nie.
3. **Waaraan het Sibu geleer om ook te dink?** Aan sy huiswerk. / Aan sy aandete. / Aan die weer. / **Aan wat sy teenstander gaan doen. ✓** / Aan die medalje.
4. **Met watter stuk het Sibu skaakmat gesê?** Met die perd. / Met die koning. / Met 'n pion. / **Met die koningin. ✓** / Met die toring.
5. **(Dinkvraag) Wat bedoel Oupa met "Ek het jou net geleer om te dink"?** Oupa wou die medalje hê. / Oupa is moeg vir skaak. / Sibu het nie regtig gewen nie. / Oupa het vir hom gespeel. / **Sibu het self gewen deur sy eie slim denke. ✓**

### Woordherkenning (10)

skaak, vervelige, koningin, rigting, beplan, teenstander, skaakklub, toernooi, kwartaal, skaakmat

### Grammatika: meervoude (5)

*Gee die meervoud – Bv. hond – honde*

1. koningin – **koninginne**
2. perd – **perde**
3. stuk – **stukke**
4. toernooi – **toernooie**
5. kwartaal – **kwartale**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. Ek en Oupa speel \_\_\_\_\_\_ op die stoep. **skaak ✓** / skaap / skaaf / skaats
2. Voor die wedstryd het ek my \_\_\_\_\_\_ se hand geskud. teenwoordig / **teenstander ✓** / teëspoed / teenoor
3. Ons span het die \_\_\_\_\_\_ gewen. tonnel / toer / **toernooi ✓** / tornado
4. Die tweede \_\_\_\_\_\_ begin in April. kwartier / kwaliteit / kwartale / **kwartaal ✓**
5. Met een slim skuif sê sy: "\_\_\_\_\_\_!" Skaakbord / **Skaakmat ✓** / Skaapmat / Skaakstuk

## Level 4 – English: Maya and the Night Sky

About 256 words, 19 sentences, 5 short paragraphs, a story with facts woven in.

### Word cards (6)

**amazement** – A feeling of great surprise. e.g. The children watched the magician in amazement. Afrikaans: "verbasing".

**galaxy** – A huge group of billions of stars in space. e.g. Our Sun is one star in the Milky Way galaxy. One galaxy, many galaxies. Afrikaans: "sterrestelsel".

**direction** – The way something is moving or pointing, such as north or south. e.g. The sign shows the direction to the beach. Afrikaans: "rigting".

**reflect** – To send back light, heat or sound from a surface. e.g. The mirror reflects my face. It reflects, it reflected. Afrikaans: "weerkaats".

**enormous** – Very, very big. e.g. An elephant is an enormous animal. Opposite: tiny. Afrikaans: "reusagtig".

**confidently** – In a way that shows you believe in yourself. e.g. Sipho walked confidently onto the stage. Afrikaans: "met selfvertroue".

*Extra cards for more support:* telescope, astronaut

### The passage

Maya's family went camping in the Karoo during the school holidays. Far away from the bright lights of the city, the night sky looked completely different. As soon as the sun set, thousands of stars began to twinkle above their tent.

Maya lay on a blanket next to her grandfather and stared upwards in amazement. "I have never seen so many stars before!" she whispered. Grandpa smiled and pointed at a long, cloudy band of light stretching across the sky. "That is the Milky Way," he explained. "It is our galaxy, and it contains billions of stars, including our own Sun."

Next, Grandpa showed her four bright stars in the shape of a kite. This is the Southern Cross, which can only be seen properly from the southern half of the world. Two very bright stars nearby, called the Pointers, lead the way to it. Long ago, sailors and travellers used these stars to find the direction of south at night.

Later, a round yellow moon rose slowly over the hills. Maya was surprised to learn that the Moon does not make any light of its own. Instead, it reflects the light of the Sun, just like a mirror. Grandpa also told her that the stars are actually enormous suns, but they are so far away that they look like tiny dots.

That night, Maya fell asleep dreaming about rockets and faraway planets. The next morning she asked her parents for a telescope for her birthday. "One day," she said confidently, "I am going to be an astronaut!"

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **Why did the night sky look different in the Karoo?** It was winter. / **It was far away from the bright city lights. ✓** / The moon was full. / It was cloudy. / They had a telescope.
2. **What is the Milky Way?** A cloud. / A road in the sky. / **Our galaxy. ✓** / A planet. / The Moon's shadow.
3. **What do the Pointers do?** They show where the Moon will rise. / They point to the Sun. / They show the time. / **They lead the way to the Southern Cross. ✓** / They point to the Karoo.
4. **Where does the Moon's light come from?** **It reflects the light of the Sun. ✓** / It makes its own light. / It comes from the stars. / It comes from the Milky Way. / It comes from fires on the Moon.
5. **(Thinking) Why did Maya ask for a telescope?** Her old one broke. / Grandpa told her to. / She wanted to watch birds. / All her friends had one. / **The stars made her want to learn more about space. ✓**

### Word recognition (10)

camping, completely, twinkle, amazement, galaxy, billions, direction, reflects, enormous, astronaut

### Grammar: adverbs (5)

*Turn the word into an adverb – e.g. slow – slowly*

1. quiet – **quietly**
2. confident – **confidently**
3. careful – **carefully**
4. happy – **happily**
5. gentle – **gently**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. The tourists stared at the waterfall in \_\_\_\_\_\_. amazing / **amazement ✓** / amazed / amusement
2. Our Sun is one of billions of stars in the \_\_\_\_\_\_. **galaxy ✓** / galaxies / gallery / galloping
3. The compass shows us which \_\_\_\_\_\_ is north. directions / director / **direction ✓** / detection
4. A still lake can \_\_\_\_\_\_ the mountains like a mirror. reflex / reflection / respect / **reflect ✓**
5. The blue whale is an \_\_\_\_\_\_ animal. enormously / **enormous ✓** / anonymous / endless

## Level 4 – English 2: The Sardine Run

Topic: Animals. About 250 words, 20 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**shoal** – A large group of fish swimming together. e.g. A shoal of fish swam past the boat. One shoal, many shoals. Afrikaans: "skool (visse)".

**coast** – The land next to the sea. e.g. We drove along the coast to Durban. Afrikaans: "kus".

**herd** – To move animals together into a group. e.g. The farmer's dog herds the sheep. I herd, I herded. Afrikaans: "bymekaarja".

**feast** – A large, special meal with lots of food. e.g. We had a feast at the wedding. One feast, many feasts. Afrikaans: "fees".

**prey** – An animal that is hunted and eaten by another animal. e.g. The mouse is prey for the owl. Afrikaans: "prooi".

**connected** – Joined or linked to something else. e.g. The two rooms are connected by a door. Afrikaans: "verbind".

*Extra cards for more support:* gannet, mystery

### The passage

Every winter, one of the greatest wildlife shows on Earth takes place along the east coast of South Africa. It is called the Sardine Run. Between May and July, huge shoals of tiny silver sardines swim north along the coast of KwaZulu-Natal. Some shoals are several kilometres long and can be seen from the air as dark patches in the water.

Scientists believe the sardines follow a band of cool water that flows up the coast in winter. For the sardines, it is a dangerous journey. Thousands of dolphins, sharks, seals and birds follow them, ready for a feast.

The common dolphins are clever hunters. They work together to herd the sardines into a tight ball near the surface. This is called a bait ball. Then the dolphins take turns darting through it to feed. From above, Cape gannets fold back their wings and dive into the water like arrows. They can plunge up to ten metres deep to catch their prey.

Divers and photographers travel from all over the world to see this amazing sight. Local fishermen also wait on the beaches with nets. When a shoal comes close to the shore, people rush into the shallow water to scoop up sardines in buckets.

Even though millions of sardines are eaten, far more survive. Their huge numbers are their best protection. The Sardine Run shows how every creature in the ocean is connected, from the tiniest fish to the biggest shark. Scientists are still studying it to understand its many mysteries.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **When does the Sardine Run happen?** Between December and February. / **Between May and July. ✓** / All year round. / Only in August. / Every second year.
2. **Why do scientists think the sardines swim north?** To lay eggs. / To escape the sharks. / **They follow a band of cool water. ✓** / To find warm water. / To find food on the beach.
3. **What is a bait ball?** A fishing net. / A ball that divers use. / A shark's meal. / **A tight ball of sardines herded by dolphins. ✓** / A kind of fish.
4. **How deep can Cape gannets dive?** One metre. / **Up to ten metres. ✓** / A hundred metres. / They can't dive. / Fifty metres.
5. **(Thinking) Why are the sardines' huge numbers their best protection?** They can fight back. / They swim faster together. / Sharks don't like big shoals. / They hide in the sand. / **There are so many that most of them survive. ✓**

### Word recognition (10)

wildlife, sardines, shoals, kilometres, dangerous, dolphins, herd, gannets, fishermen, mysteries

### Grammar: prefixes un- and dis- (5)

*Add un- or dis- to make the opposite – e.g. kind – unkind*

1. happy – **unhappy**
2. appear – **disappear**
3. agree – **disagree**
4. safe – **unsafe**
5. like – **dislike**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. A \_\_\_\_\_\_ of fish swam under the boat. **shoal ✓** / shawl / shovel / shoals
2. We drove along the \_\_\_\_\_\_ from Cape Town to Durban. cost / **coast ✓** / coats / toast
3. The sheepdog helps to \_\_\_\_\_\_ the sheep into the pen. heard / hard / **herd ✓** / herds
4. The lion crept slowly towards its \_\_\_\_\_\_. pray / play / preys / **prey ✓**
5. All living things on Earth are \_\_\_\_\_\_. collected / **connected ✓** / corrected / connecting

## Level 4 – English 3: The Wheelchair Basketball Match

Topic: Sport and games. About 252 words, 20 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**expect** – To think that something will happen. e.g. I expect it will rain later. I expect, I expected. Afrikaans: "verwag".

**ordinary** – Normal and usual; not special. e.g. It was an ordinary Monday. Afrikaans: "gewoon".

**slant** – To lean or slope to one side. e.g. The old tree slants towards the river. It slants, it slanted. Afrikaans: "skuins staan".

**tip over** – To fall onto its side. e.g. The glass tipped over and the milk spilt. Afrikaans: "omval".

**erupt** – To burst out suddenly, like a volcano. e.g. The crowd erupted when we scored. It erupts, it erupted. Afrikaans: "uitbars".

**respect** – A feeling that someone is good or brave and deserves admiration. e.g. We have great respect for our coach. Afrikaans: "respek".

*Extra cards for more support:* rim, spare

### The passage

When Karabo's cousin Sipho invited him to watch a basketball match, Karabo expected an ordinary game. But when he arrived at the sports hall, all the players were in wheelchairs. Sipho had been born with legs that didn't work, but that had never stopped him.

The wheelchairs were not like normal ones. They were light and low, with wheels that slanted inwards so they could turn quickly without tipping over. Players pushed the wheels with their hands and had to bounce the ball at the same time. The rules were almost the same as in ordinary basketball.

The game was fast and exciting. Chairs crashed into each other with loud bangs, and sometimes a player tipped right over. Each time, they pushed themselves back up and carried on playing. Sipho was the team's best shooter. With seconds left, he spun his chair around, aimed and threw the ball high into the air.

The whole hall held its breath as the ball swirled around the rim. Then it dropped through the net, and the crowd erupted with cheers. Sipho's team had won by two points!

After the match, Sipho let Karabo try a spare wheelchair. It was much harder than it looked. Within minutes his arms were burning, and he could barely steer in a straight line. "How do you do this for a whole game?" he gasped. Sipho laughed. "Practice, practice and more practice." Karabo left the hall with a new respect for his cousin and a new favourite sport.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **What surprised Karabo when he arrived at the hall?** The hall was empty. / **All the players were in wheelchairs. ✓** / The players were very tall. / There was no ball. / Sipho wasn't playing.
2. **Why did the wheels slant inwards?** To look cool. / To go faster downhill. / To carry the ball. / **To turn quickly without tipping over. ✓** / To make them lighter.
3. **What did the players do when they tipped over?** They gave up. / They called the coach. / **They pushed themselves back up and carried on playing. ✓** / They changed chairs. / They left the game.
4. **By how many points did Sipho's team win?** One. / **Two. ✓** / Five. / Ten. / They lost.
5. **(Thinking) Why did Karabo leave with a new respect for his cousin?** Sipho gave him a present. / Sipho was taller than him. / **He discovered how hard wheelchair basketball really is. ✓** / Sipho scored every point. / He didn't like basketball.

### Word recognition (10)

cousin, basketball, ordinary, wheelchairs, slanted, bounce, exciting, crashed, erupted, respect

### Grammar: past tense (5)

*Give the past tense – e.g. run – ran*

1. spin – **spun**
2. hold – **held**
3. leave – **left**
4. think – **thought**
5. let – **let**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. I \_\_\_\_\_\_ Gran will visit us on Sunday. **expect ✓** / except / expert / expects
2. It was just an \_\_\_\_\_\_ day at school. orderly / **ordinary ✓** / ordinal / extraordinary
3. Be careful, or the jug will \_\_\_\_\_\_. tidy up / tick off / **tip over ✓** / top up
4. The fans \_\_\_\_\_\_ in cheers when the goal was scored. interrupted / erupt / eroded / **erupted ✓**
5. We should treat everyone with \_\_\_\_\_\_. respond / **respect ✓** / inspect / suspect

## Level 4 – English 4: The Time Capsule

Topic: Adventure and make-believe. About 255 words, 21 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**announcement** – Something said publicly to tell people news. e.g. The principal made an announcement at assembly. One announcement, many announcements. Afrikaans: "aankondiging".

**bury** – To put something in a hole in the ground and cover it. e.g. The dog buried its bone in the garden. I bury, I buried. Afrikaans: "begrawe".

**time capsule** – A container of things from today that is hidden for people in the future to find. e.g. We put our drawings in a time capsule. Afrikaans: "tydkapsule".

**seal** – To close something tightly so that nothing can get in or out. e.g. Seal the envelope before posting it. I seal, I sealed. Afrikaans: "verseël".

**old-fashioned** – From long ago and not modern any more. e.g. Grandpa has an old-fashioned radio. Afrikaans: "outyds".

**label** – To write a name on something so people know what it is. e.g. Label your lunchbox with your name. I label, I labelled. Afrikaans: "merk".

*Extra cards for more support:* cassette, wax

### The passage

When Riverside Primary celebrated its hundredth birthday, the principal made an exciting announcement. Fifty years earlier, pupils had buried a time capsule under the big oak tree on the field. Now it was time to dig it up.

The whole school gathered around as two gardeners carefully dug into the hard ground. After an hour, a spade struck metal. Slowly, they lifted out a rusty tin box, its lid sealed with thick wax. Nadia, who was in Grade 5, was chosen to help open it. Her hands shook with excitement as she pulled off the lid.

Inside were treasures from a very different time. There was a black-and-white photo of a class wearing long socks and old-fashioned school hats. There was a newspaper that had cost only two cents, and a comic with a torn cover. A small cassette tape was labelled "Our favourite songs". Nobody in Nadia's class had ever seen one before!

At the bottom was a letter written by the children of long ago. "We wonder if you still have to learn your times tables," it said. "We wonder if cars can fly yet, and if everyone has a robot to do their homework." The whole school burst out laughing.

That afternoon, Nadia's class decided to make a new time capsule. They added a class photo, a drawing of a tablet, a list of their favourite games and a letter to the future. On the envelope, Nadia wrote: "Do not open for fifty years." She wondered what the children of the future would think of them.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **How long ago was the time capsule buried?** Ten years ago. / A hundred years ago. / **Fifty years ago. ✓** / Twenty years ago. / Last year.
2. **Where was it buried?** Under the school hall. / **Under the big oak tree on the field. ✓** / Behind the classrooms. / In the principal's garden. / Next to the gate.
3. **How was the lid sealed?** With tape. / With glue. / With a lock. / **With thick wax. ✓** / With nails.
4. **What had nobody in Nadia's class seen before?** A newspaper. / A comic. / A photo. / A school hat. / **A cassette tape. ✓**
5. **(Thinking) Why did the school burst out laughing at the letter?** **The children of long ago guessed things that didn't come true, like flying cars. ✓** / The letter was badly written. / The letter was very rude. / The principal told a joke. / The letter was upside down.

### Word recognition (10)

celebrated, hundredth, announcement, capsule, buried, gardeners, sealed, excitement, old-fashioned, labelled

### Grammar: owning with 's (5)

*Rewrite with 's – e.g. the bag of Tom – Tom's bag*

1. the hat of the boy – **the boy's hat**
2. the tail of the dog – **the dog's tail**
3. the car of Dad – **Dad's car**
4. the name of the school – **the school's name**
5. the lid of the box – **the box's lid**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. The principal made an important \_\_\_\_\_\_. amusement / **announcement ✓** / announcer / annoyance
2. Squirrels \_\_\_\_\_\_ nuts to eat in winter. berry / blurry / **bury ✓** / buries
3. \_\_\_\_\_\_ the jar tightly so the air stays out. **Seal ✓** / Steal / Sell / Seals
4. Grandpa still uses an \_\_\_\_\_\_ radio. old-timer / olden / **old-fashioned ✓** / old-fashion
5. Please \_\_\_\_\_\_ each box with your name. table / level / labels / **label ✓**

## Level 4 – English 5: After the Fire

Topic: Nature and outdoors. About 248 words, 23 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**glow** – A soft, steady light. e.g. We saw the glow of the city lights from far away. Afrikaans: "gloed".

**release** – To let something go or set it free. e.g. The children released the bird from the cage. I release, I released. Afrikaans: "vrylaat".

**crack open** – To break open with a split. e.g. The egg cracked open and a chick came out. Afrikaans: "oopbars".

**fertiliser** – Something added to soil to help plants grow. e.g. Gran puts fertiliser on her roses. Afrikaans: "kunsmis".

**shoot** – A new, young part of a plant that grows up out of the ground. e.g. Green shoots appeared in spring. One shoot, many shoots. Afrikaans: "loot".

**unattended** – Left alone with nobody watching it. e.g. Never leave the stove unattended. Afrikaans: "sonder toesig".

*Extra cards for more support:* disaster, chemical

### The passage

One summer night, Liam watched an orange glow on the mountain behind his home in Cape Town. A veld fire was burning through the fynbos. Firefighters and helicopters worked through the night to keep it away from the houses.

A week later, Liam and his father walked up the burnt mountainside. Everything was black, and the air still smelt of smoke. "It's so sad," said Liam. "Everything is dead." His father shook his head. "Not everything. Fynbos actually needs fire to stay healthy."

He explained that many fynbos plants only release their seeds after a fire. Some seeds need the heat of the flames to crack open. Others are woken up by chemicals in the smoke. The ash also acts like fertiliser, adding food to the soil. Without a fire every ten to twenty years, the old bushes grow too thick, and new plants can't grow.

A few months later, they climbed the mountain again. Liam couldn't believe his eyes. Bright green shoots were pushing up through the black soil. Fire lilies, with their bright red flowers, bloomed everywhere. Sunbirds and bees were busy visiting the flowers.

However, his father warned that fires started by people are dangerous. If fires happen too often, the young plants don't have time to grow up and make seeds. That is why we must never throw away burning matches or leave campfires unattended. Liam realised that nature is cleverer than he had thought. Even after a disaster, it knows how to heal itself.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **What did Liam see on the mountain one night?** Lightning. / Car lights. / **The orange glow of a veld fire. ✓** / Fireworks. / A full moon.
2. **What did Liam think when he saw the burnt mountain?** It looked beautiful. / **Everything was dead. ✓** / It would soon rain. / The fire was still burning. / It was a good place to play.
3. **How are some seeds woken up after a fire?** By the rain. / By birds. / By the wind. / By the sun. / **By chemicals in the smoke. ✓**
4. **Which flowers bloomed everywhere a few months later?** Roses. / **Fire lilies. ✓** / Sunflowers. / Daisies. / Proteas.
5. **(Thinking) Why are fires started by people dangerous, even though fynbos needs fire?** Fynbos hates people. / **They can happen too often, before the young plants have made seeds. ✓** / They are always bigger. / They burn only at night. / They never go out.

### Word recognition (10)

firefighters, helicopters, mountainside, release, flames, chemicals, fertiliser, shoots, unattended, disaster

### Grammar: adding -less (5)

*Add -less – e.g. care – careless*

1. help – **helpless**
2. hope – **hopeless**
3. harm – **harmless**
4. use – **useless**
5. fear – **fearless**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. We saw the orange \_\_\_\_\_\_ of the fire from far away. glue / **glow ✓** / grow / glove
2. The rangers \_\_\_\_\_\_ the rescued owl back into the wild. relaxed / replaced / **released ✓** / reversed
3. Gran puts \_\_\_\_\_\_ on her vegetables so they grow well. fertile / fertility / filler / **fertiliser ✓**
4. Tiny green \_\_\_\_\_\_ pushed up through the soil. **shoots ✓** / shouts / shorts / shoot
5. Never leave a campfire \_\_\_\_\_\_. unattached / attended / **unattended ✓** / unintended

## Level 4 – English 6: Making a Rainbow

Topic: How things work. About 250 words, 21 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**bend** – To change direction or become curved. e.g. The road bends to the left. It bends, it bent. Afrikaans: "buig".

**prism** – A clear glass or plastic shape that splits white light into colours. e.g. The prism made a rainbow on the wall. One prism, many prisms. Afrikaans: "prisma".

**order** – The way things are arranged, one after another. e.g. Put the words in alphabetical order. Afrikaans: "volgorde".

**violet** – A bluish-purple colour. e.g. The violet flowers smelt sweet. Afrikaans: "violet".

**windowsill** – The flat shelf at the bottom of a window. e.g. The cat sleeps on the windowsill. One windowsill, many windowsills. Afrikaans: "vensterbank".

**shimmer** – To shine with a soft, wavy light. e.g. The lake shimmered in the moonlight. It shimmers, it shimmered. Afrikaans: "glinster".

*Extra cards for more support:* mist, hosepipe

### The passage

After a sudden summer storm, a huge rainbow stretched across the sky above Ayesha's house. Her little brother Zain asked where rainbows come from. Ayesha wasn't sure, so she decided to find out.

At the library, she found a book about light. It explained that sunlight looks white, but it is actually made up of many colours mixed together. When sunlight passes through a raindrop, the light bends. Each colour bends by a slightly different amount, so the colours spread out. The raindrop acts like a tiny prism.

The book also explained that rainbows always have the same order of colours. Red is on the outside, followed by orange, yellow, green, blue, indigo and violet. Some people remember the order with the name "Roy G. Biv", using the first letter of each colour. To see a rainbow, the sun must be behind you and the rain in front of you.

Ayesha wanted to show Zain how it works. On a sunny morning, she filled a glass with water and put it on the windowsill. She placed a sheet of white paper on the floor below it. Slowly, she moved the glass until the sunlight shone through it. Suddenly, a small rainbow appeared on the paper!

Zain clapped his hands and tried to catch the colours. Then they went outside and sprayed a fine mist from the hosepipe, with their backs to the sun. Another rainbow shimmered in the air. "Now I can make a rainbow whenever I want," laughed Zain.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **What is sunlight actually made of?** Only yellow light. / **Many colours mixed together. ✓** / Tiny drops of water. / White paint. / Heat and nothing else.
2. **What does a raindrop act like?** A mirror. / A magnet. / **A tiny prism. ✓** / A lamp. / A sponge.
3. **Which colour is on the outside of a rainbow?** Violet. / Blue. / Green. / **Red. ✓** / Yellow.
4. **Where must the sun be for you to see a rainbow?** In front of you. / Above your head. / **Behind you. ✓** / Behind the clouds. / Under the horizon.
5. **(Thinking) Why did Ayesha do the experiment with the glass of water?** To water the plants. / To make Zain wet. / To clean the window. / For a school test. / **To show Zain how a rainbow forms. ✓**

### Word recognition (10)

rainbow, library, sunlight, raindrop, slightly, prism, orange, violet, windowsill, shimmered

### Grammar: irregular comparisons (5)

*Complete – e.g. good – better – best*

1. bad – **worse** – **worst**
2. many – **more** – **most**
3. little – **less** – **least**
4. far – **farther** – **farthest** *(accept: further, furthest)*
5. well – **better** – **best**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. Light \_\_\_\_\_\_ when it passes through water. **bends ✓** / bands / blends / bend
2. Hold the \_\_\_\_\_\_ up to the sunlight to see the colours. prison / **prism ✓** / prim / prize
3. Put the numbers in the correct \_\_\_\_\_\_. odour / **order ✓** / older / orders
4. The cat is asleep on the \_\_\_\_\_\_. window / windmill / windscreen / **windowsill ✓**
5. The sea \_\_\_\_\_\_ in the moonlight. shivered / shimmer / simmered / **shimmered ✓**

## Level 4 – English 7: From Bean to Bar

Topic: Food and cooking. About 253 words, 21 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**equator** – An imaginary line around the middle of the Earth, where it is very hot. e.g. Countries near the equator are hot all year. Afrikaans: "ewenaar".

**pod** – A long case that holds the seeds of some plants. e.g. Peas grow inside a green pod. One pod, many pods. Afrikaans: "peul".

**sprout** – To start to grow. e.g. The beans sprouted after a few days. It sprouts, it sprouted. Afrikaans: "uitspruit".

**ferment** – To change slowly because of tiny living things, like yeast. e.g. Yoghurt is made when milk ferments. It ferments, it fermented. Afrikaans: "fermenteer".

**bitter** – A strong, sharp taste that is not sweet. e.g. Dark coffee tastes bitter. Opposite: sweet. Afrikaans: "bitter".

**fairly** – In a way that is right and equal for everyone. e.g. The teacher shared the sweets fairly. Afrikaans: "regverdig".

*Extra cards for more support:* machete, roast

### The passage

When Tumi bit into her chocolate bar, she suddenly wondered where chocolate actually comes from. She was surprised to learn that it starts as a seed growing on a tree.

Chocolate is made from the seeds of the cacao tree, which grows in hot, rainy places near the equator. More than half of the world's cacao comes from two countries in West Africa: Côte d'Ivoire and Ghana. The seeds, called cocoa beans, grow inside large pods that sprout straight from the tree trunk. When the pods are ripe, they turn yellow, orange or red.

Farmers cut the pods open with a machete and scoop out the beans, which are covered in a sweet white pulp. The beans are left to ferment under banana leaves for about a week. This is when they begin to develop their chocolatey flavour. Then they are spread out in the sun to dry.

The dried beans are shipped to factories all over the world. There, they are roasted, cracked open and ground into a thick, dark paste. Sugar, milk and cocoa butter are mixed in to make the smooth chocolate that we know. Surprisingly, raw cocoa beans taste very bitter, not sweet at all!

Tumi also learnt that many cacao farmers earn very little money. Some chocolate has a special label showing that the farmers were paid fairly. The next time Tumi went to the shop with her mom, she looked for that label. Her chocolate tasted even sweeter, knowing that the farmers had been treated fairly.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **What is chocolate made from?** Sugar cane. / Milk and butter. / **The seeds of the cacao tree. ✓** / Banana leaves. / Coffee beans.
2. **Which two countries grow more than half of the world's cacao?** Brazil and Mexico. / South Africa and Kenya. / **Côte d'Ivoire and Ghana. ✓** / India and China. / Egypt and Morocco.
3. **Where do the cacao pods grow?** Under the ground. / On the tips of the branches. / On small bushes. / **Straight from the tree trunk. ✓** / In the roots.
4. **How do raw cocoa beans taste?** Very sweet. / Salty. / Like milk. / **Very bitter. ✓** / Like bananas.
5. **(Thinking) Why did Tumi's chocolate taste "even sweeter"?** It had more sugar in it. / It was a bigger bar. / It was on special. / Her mom paid for it. / **She felt good knowing that the farmers had been paid fairly. ✓**

### Word recognition (10)

chocolate, equator, countries, cacao, pods, ferment, flavour, factories, bitter, fairly

### Grammar: words that sound the same (5)

*Choose the right word*

1. The farmers cut \_\_\_ pods open. (their / there) – **their**
2. The beans dry in the \_\_\_. (son / sun) – **sun**
3. Tumi went \_\_\_ the shop. (to / two) – **to**
4. \_\_\_ does chocolate come from? (Wear / Where) – **Where**
5. Raw beans are \_\_\_ bitter to eat. (to / too) – **too**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. Countries near the \_\_\_\_\_\_ are hot all year. equal / **equator ✓** / elevator / equation
2. The seeds grow inside a large \_\_\_\_\_\_. pot / pad / pods / **pod ✓**
3. Dark chocolate tastes a little \_\_\_\_\_\_. better / butter / **bitter ✓** / bitten
4. The teacher shared the apples \_\_\_\_\_\_. **fairly ✓** / fairy / fairer / firmly
5. Milk \_\_\_\_\_\_ to make yoghurt. ferment / fragments / fermenting / **ferments ✓**

## Level 4 – English 8: The Secret Life of Bones

Topic: My body and health. About 255 words, 22 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**curious** – Wanting to know or learn about something. e.g. The curious kitten sniffed the box. Afrikaans: "nuuskierig".

**skeleton** – All the bones in a body, joined together. e.g. We saw a dinosaur skeleton at the museum. One skeleton, many skeletons. Afrikaans: "geraamte".

**thigh** – The top part of your leg, between your hip and your knee. e.g. The runner hurt her thigh muscle. Afrikaans: "dy".

**marrow** – The soft material inside some bones that makes blood cells. e.g. Bone marrow makes new blood every day. Afrikaans: "murg".

**calcium** – A mineral in milk and other foods that keeps your bones and teeth strong. e.g. Cheese is full of calcium. Afrikaans: "kalsium".

**vitamin** – Something in food or sunlight that your body needs to stay healthy. e.g. Oranges are full of vitamin C. One vitamin, many vitamins. Afrikaans: "vitamien".

*Extra cards for more support:* wrist, trampoline

### The passage

When Leo broke his wrist falling off a trampoline, he spent six weeks in a cast. During that time, he became curious about the bones inside his body.

He learnt that an adult's skeleton has 206 bones. Amazingly, a baby is born with around 300! As babies grow, some of their bones slowly join together. The smallest bone is in your ear and is about the size of a grain of rice. The longest is the thigh bone, which runs from your hip to your knee.

Many people think bones are dry and dead, like sticks. In fact, bones are alive. They have blood flowing through them, and they can grow and repair themselves. Inside some bones is a soft, jelly-like material called marrow. Marrow makes millions of new blood cells every second.

To stay strong, bones need calcium, which is found in milk, cheese, yoghurt and green vegetables. Bones also need exercise. When you run, jump or dance, your bones become thicker and stronger. Sunlight helps too, because it helps your body make vitamin D, which your body needs to use calcium.

When Leo's cast finally came off, his wrist looked thin and weak. The doctor said this was normal and gave him exercises to do. Leo squeezed a soft ball every day and drank a glass of milk with every meal. Within a month, his wrist was as strong as before. "My bones are cleverer than I thought," said Leo. He still bounces on the trampoline, but a little more carefully now.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **How many bones does an adult's skeleton have?** 106. / 300. / **206. ✓** / 1 000. / 26.
2. **Where is the smallest bone in your body?** In your toe. / **In your ear. ✓** / In your finger. / In your nose. / In your wrist.
3. **What does bone marrow make?** Calcium. / Muscles. / **New blood cells. ✓** / Hair. / Vitamin D.
4. **Which foods contain calcium?** Sweets and chips. / Bread and jam. / Meat and rice. / **Milk, cheese, yoghurt and green vegetables. ✓** / Apples and oranges.
5. **(Thinking) Why did Leo's wrist look thin and weak when the cast came off?** The cast was too tight. / He broke it again. / **It hadn't been used or exercised for six weeks. ✓** / The doctor made a mistake. / He didn't drink enough water.

### Word recognition (10)

trampoline, curious, skeleton, amazingly, thigh, alive, marrow, calcium, exercise, vitamin

### Grammar: plurals ending in -ves (5)

*Give the plural – e.g. leaf – leaves*

1. knife – **knives**
2. half – **halves**
3. life – **lives**
4. calf – **calves**
5. wolf – **wolves**

### Vocabulary (5)

2. We saw a whale \_\_\_\_\_\_ at the museum. skeletons / skeptic / skillet / **skeleton ✓**
3. The \_\_\_\_\_\_ bone is the longest bone in your body. **thigh ✓** / thing / thick / tight
4. Cheese and milk are full of \_\_\_\_\_\_. calendar / **calcium ✓** / calculate / cactus
5. Oranges contain lots of \_\_\_\_\_\_ C. violin / victim / vitamins / **vitamin ✓**

## Level 4 – English 9: Tumelo the Orphan Rhino

Topic: Animals. About 251 words, 21 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**ranger** – A person whose job is to look after a nature reserve and its animals. e.g. The ranger showed us lion tracks. One ranger, many rangers. Afrikaans: "veldwagter".

**orphanage** – A home that looks after young people or animals that have lost their parents. e.g. The elephant orphanage cares for baby elephants. Afrikaans: "weeshuis".

**carer** – Someone who looks after a person or animal that needs help. e.g. The carer fed the baby monkey. One carer, many carers. Afrikaans: "versorger".

**high-pitched** – Very high and thin in sound. e.g. The kitten gave a high-pitched meow. Afrikaans: "skril".

**keratin** – The tough material that hair, nails, hooves and horns are made of. e.g. Your fingernails are made of keratin. Afrikaans: "keratien".

**trot** – To move faster than walking but slower than running. e.g. The horse trotted around the field. It trots, it trotted. Afrikaans: "draf".

*Extra cards for more support:* waterhole, release

### The passage

One morning, rangers in the Kruger National Park found a baby rhino standing alone beside a waterhole. It was only a few weeks old, and its mother was nowhere to be seen. Without her milk and protection, the little calf would not survive.

The rangers carefully loaded the calf onto a truck and drove it to a rhino orphanage. There, a team of carers named him Tumelo, which means "faith" in Setswana. Tumelo was frightened and cried with a high-pitched squeak. To calm him, one carer stayed with him day and night, just as his mother would have.

Baby rhinos drink a lot of milk. Tumelo needed a special milk mixture from a giant bottle every three hours. Within a few weeks, he was following his carers everywhere, like a large, grey puppy. He loved rolling in mud, which protects a rhino's skin from the sun and insects.

Rhinos are in danger because some people hunt them illegally for their horns. Their horns are made of keratin, the same material as our hair and fingernails. The carers hope that one day there will be enough safe places for all rhinos to live freely.

After three years, Tumelo was big and strong enough to return to the wild. On the day of his release, the carers watched with tears in their eyes as he trotted into the bush. Rangers still watch over him from a distance. Tumelo, whose name means faith, has shown that there is still hope for rhinos.

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **Where did the rangers find the baby rhino?** In a river. / On a farm road. / **Beside a waterhole in the Kruger National Park. ✓** / In a zoo. / Near a town.
2. **What does the name Tumelo mean?** Strong. / Brave. / Little one. / **Faith. ✓** / Friend.
3. **How often did Tumelo need a bottle of milk?** Once a day. / Every hour. / **Every three hours. ✓** / Twice a week. / Only at night.
4. **What are rhino horns made of?** Bone. / Ivory. / Stone. / Wood. / **Keratin, like our hair and nails. ✓**
5. **(Thinking) Why did one carer stay with Tumelo day and night?** To stop him running away. / To count his breaths. / **To comfort him, just as his mother would have. ✓** / Because there was no room at home. / To take photos.

### Word recognition (10)

rangers, waterhole, protection, orphanage, frightened, high-pitched, mixture, protects, keratin, release

### Grammar: owning with ' after plurals (5)

*Rewrite with ' – e.g. the toys of the girls – the girls' toys*

1. the truck of the rangers – **the rangers' truck**
2. the horns of the rhinos – **the rhinos' horns**
3. the names of the babies – **the babies' names**
4. the milk of the calves – **the calves' milk**
5. the voices of the children – **the children's voices** *(children is already plural, so it takes 's)*

### Vocabulary (5)

1. The \_\_\_\_\_\_ showed us where the lions were sleeping. range / danger / **ranger ✓** / manger
2. The baby elephant lives at an animal \_\_\_\_\_\_. orphan / **orphanage ✓** / orange / orchard
3. The kitten gave a \_\_\_\_\_\_ squeak. **high-pitched ✓** / high-flying / pitched / high-heeled
4. Your hair and nails are made of \_\_\_\_\_\_. kerosene / karate / carotene / **keratin ✓**
5. The horse \_\_\_\_\_\_ across the field. trod / **trotted ✓** / trotter / rotted

## Level 4 – English 10: Lost in the Maize Maze

Topic: Adventure and make-believe. About 254 words, 22 sentences, 5 short paragraphs.

### Word cards (6)

**maze** – A puzzle made of paths or walls where you have to find the way through. e.g. We got lost in the hedge maze. One maze, many mazes. Afrikaans: "doolhof".

**narrow** – Not wide. e.g. The path between the rocks was very narrow. Opposite: wide. Afrikaans: "smal".

**rustle** – A soft, dry sound like leaves or paper moving. e.g. The leaves rustled in the wind. It rustles, it rustled. Afrikaans: "ritsel".

**scarecrow** – A figure dressed in old clothes that farmers put in fields to scare birds away. e.g. The scarecrow wore a straw hat. One scarecrow, many scarecrows. Afrikaans: "voëlverskrikker".

**dead end** – A path or road that doesn't lead anywhere. e.g. We turned back because the street was a dead end. Afrikaans: "doodloopstraat".

**strategy** – A clever plan for reaching a goal. e.g. Our team had a good strategy for winning. One strategy, many strategies. Afrikaans: "strategie".

*Extra cards for more support:* eventually, panting

### The passage

For Nomsa's birthday, her family visited a giant maize maze on a farm outside Pretoria. The farmer had cut narrow paths through a field of maize plants that were taller than any adult. "The first team to reach the tower in the middle wins a prize," he announced.

Nomsa and her cousin Thabo set off confidently. At first it was fun, turning left and right between the rustling green walls. But after twenty minutes, they had walked past the same scarecrow three times. "We're going around in circles," groaned Thabo.

Nomsa remembered something she had read in a book about mazes. "If you keep one hand touching the wall and never let go, you'll eventually find your way," she said. It might not be the shortest route, but it would stop them from getting completely lost. They chose the right-hand wall and set off again, trailing their fingers along the leaves.

The path twisted and turned through dead ends and long corners. Twice they had to walk back the way they had come. Thabo wanted to give up, but Nomsa kept her hand firmly on the wall. At last, they turned a corner and saw the wooden tower rising above the maize.

They climbed to the top, panting and laughing. From up there, they could see the whole maze spread out below them like a green puzzle. They weren't the first team, but the farmer gave them a prize anyway: two ice-cold cooldrinks for the cleverest strategy. "Next year," said Thabo, "I'm bringing that book!"

### Comprehension (5 questions, ✓ = correct)

1. **What was the maze made of?** Hedges. / Stone walls. / **Tall maize plants. ✓** / Hay bales. / Wooden fences.
2. **How did they know they were going around in circles?** Thabo saw a map. / They heard the farmer. / **They walked past the same scarecrow three times. ✓** / Nomsa's watch told them. / They saw their own footprints.
3. **What was Nomsa's plan?** To climb over the maize. / To shout for help. / To follow another team. / **To keep one hand touching the wall. ✓** / To run as fast as possible.
4. **What prize did the farmer give them?** A medal. / A trophy. / A bag of maize. / Free tickets. / **Two ice-cold cooldrinks. ✓**
5. **(Thinking) Why did the farmer give them a prize even though they weren't first?** It was Nomsa's birthday. / He felt sorry for them. / **He was impressed by their clever strategy. ✓** / They paid him. / Everyone got a prize.

### Word recognition (10)

birthday, maize, maze, narrow, confidently, rustling, scarecrow, eventually, strategy, cooldrinks

### Grammar: adding -ful (5)

*Add -ful – e.g. help – helpful*

1. hope – **hopeful**
2. care – **careful**
3. peace – **peaceful**
4. wonder – **wonderful**
5. colour – **colourful**

### Vocabulary (5)

1. We found our way through the hedge \_\_\_\_\_\_. maize / **maze ✓** / mace / mazes
2. The path was so \_\_\_\_\_\_ that we walked in single file. arrow / marrow / **narrow ✓** / narrower
3. The dry leaves \_\_\_\_\_\_ under our feet. rusted / hustled / wrestled / **rustled ✓**
4. The farmer put a \_\_\_\_\_\_ in the field to frighten the crows. **scarecrow ✓** / scarecrows / scarf / scooter
5. We need a good \_\_\_\_\_\_ to win the game. strategies / tragedy / straggly / **strategy ✓**

## Level 5 – Afrikaans: Elke druppel tel

About 341 words, 21 sentences, 5 paragraphs, informative and advice-style.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**skaars** – Wanneer daar baie min van iets is. Dit is moeilik om te kry. Bv. In die winter is vars aarbeie skaars en duur. Teenoorgestelde: volop. Engels: "scarce".

**droogte** – 'n Lang tyd wanneer dit glad nie of baie min reën nie. Die grond word droog en die damme raak leeg. Bv. Tydens die droogte het baie boere se vee honger gely. Een droogte, baie droogtes. Engels: "drought".

**verdamp** – Wanneer water warm word en as damp in die lug verdwyn. Bv. Die plassie water het in die son verdamp. Dit verdamp, dit het verdamp. Engels: "evaporate".

**inheems** – Plante of diere wat van nature in 'n land voorkom en nie van elders ingebring is nie. Bv. Die protea is 'n inheemse blom van Suid-Afrika. Teenoorgestelde: uitheems. Engels: "indigenous".

**kosbaar** – Baie waardevol en belangrik. Iets wat jy mooi moet oppas. Bv. Ouma se ring is baie kosbaar vir haar. Engels: "precious".

**onskuldig** – Wanneer iets lyk asof dit geen skade kan doen nie, of wanneer iemand niks verkeerd gedoen het nie. Bv. Die hondjie het onskuldig gelyk, maar hy het die skoen opgekou. Engels: "innocent / harmless".

*Extra cards for more support:* andersins, boonop

### Die leesstuk

Het jy al ooit gewonder waar die water vandaan kom wat so maklik uit jou kraan loop? Dit lyk dalk asof daar altyd genoeg sal wees, maar in Suid-Afrika is water eintlik baie skaars. Ons land kry minder reën as baie ander lande, en droogtes kom gereeld voor. Daarom moet elkeen van ons leer om water slim te gebruik.

Die meeste water in 'n huis word in die badkamer gebruik, en dit is juis daar waar jy die grootste verskil kan maak. Wie het nou eintlik 'n vol bad nodig as 'n vinnige stort net so lekker is? Probeer om jou stort korter as vyf minute te hou, en draai die kraan toe terwyl jy jou tande borsel. Glo dit of nie, maar 'n oop kraan kan elke minuut tot ses liter skoon water in die drein laat afloop!

Ook in die kombuis kan die hele gesin help. Was die groente in 'n bak in plaas van onder 'n lopende kraan, en gooi daardie water later oor die plante in die tuin. Skakel die skottelgoedwasser of wasmasjien eers aan wanneer dit heeltemal vol is. 'n Lekkende kraan lyk dalk onskuldig, maar daardie klein druppeltjies kan in een jaar duisende liters water mors. Vra dus vir Pa of Ma om lekkende krane so gou moontlik reg te maak.

In die tuin is daar ook slim planne. Gee die plante vroeg in die oggend of laat in die middag water, want in die hitte van die dag verdamp die water voordat die wortels dit kan opneem. 'n Reënwatertenk langs die huis vang die reën op wat andersins sou wegloop. Inheemse plante, soos vetplante en aalwyne, het boonop baie minder water nodig as 'n groen grasperk.

Jy dink dalk dat een kind nie juis 'n verskil kan maak nie. Maar as elke kind in jou skool elke dag net 'n paar liter spaar, is dit binne 'n jaar genoeg om verskeie swembaddens vol te maak! Water is kosbaar, en elke druppel tel. Begin vandag, en wys vir jou vriende hoe maklik dit is!

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Waarom is water skaars in Suid-Afrika?** Ons land het te veel mense. / **Ons land kry minder reën as baie ander lande. ✓** / Ons land se damme is te klein. / Ons land se water is te vuil. / Ons land verkoop water aan ander lande.
2. **Hoeveel water kan 'n oop kraan elke minuut laat afloop?** Tot twee / vier / **ses ✓** / tien / twintig liter.
3. **Waarom moet jy nie die tuin in die middag se hitte natmaak nie?** Want die plante slaap dan. / Want die water is te warm vir die plante. / **Want die water verdamp voordat die wortels dit kan opneem. ✓** / Want die grond word te hard. / Want die bure kla.
4. **(Dinkvraag) Watter plante sal die beste in 'n droë tuin groei?** 'n Groen grasperk / **Aalwyne en vetplante ✓** / Rose en varings / Groot bome met baie blare / Waterlelies
5. **(Dinkvraag) Wat is die hoofgedagte van die leesstuk?** Swem is gesond. / Stort is lekkerder as bad. / **Almal kan help om water te spaar. ✓** / Tuinmaak is lekker. / Krane lek dikwels.

### Woordherkenning (10)

skaars, droogte, verdamp, inheemse, kosbaar, onskuldig, skottelgoedwasser, reënwatertenk, aalwyne, druppeltjies

### Grammatika: trappe van vergelyking (5)

*Voltooi die trappe van vergelyking – Bv. klein – kleiner – kleinste*

1. min – **minder** – **minste**
2. groot – **groter** – **grootste**
3. maklik – **makliker** – **maklikste**
4. vinnig – **vinniger** – **vinnigste**
5. goed – **beter** – **beste**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. In die somer is daar dikwels 'n \_\_\_\_\_\_, en dan moet boere hul diere ekstra voer. droogtes / **droogte ✓** / droë / drooggoed
2. Die water in die bak het in die warm son \_\_\_\_\_\_. verdam / vermors / **verdamp ✓** / verdamping
3. Die protea is 'n \_\_\_\_\_\_ blom van Suid-Afrika. inheems / **inheemse ✓** / heemse / uitheemse
4. Oupa se ou horlosie is baie \_\_\_\_\_\_ vir hom. kosbare / **kosbaar ✓** / koskas / kostelik
5. Gedurende die droogte was vars water op die plaas baie \_\_\_\_\_\_. **skaars ✓** / skaar / skerp / skaarste

## Level 5 – Afrikaans 2: Klein diertjies, groot werk

Topic: Animals. About 346 words, 22 sentences, 5 paragraphs, informative and persuasive.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**bestuiwing** – Wanneer stuifmeel van een blom na 'n ander gedra word sodat die plant vrugte en saad kan maak. Bv. Bye help met die bestuiwing van appelbome. Engels: "pollination".

**stuifmeel** – 'n Fyn, geel poeier in blomme wat nodig is sodat nuwe plante kan groei. Bv. Die by se pote was geel van die stuifmeel. Engels: "pollen".

**parasiet** – 'n Klein dier of plant wat op of in 'n ander lewende ding woon en daarvan leef. Bv. Bosluise is parasiete wat bloed suig. Een parasiet, baie parasiete. Engels: "parasite".

**boord** – 'n Stuk grond waar baie vrugtebome geplant is. Bv. Ons het appels in die boord gepluk. Een boord, baie boorde. Engels: "orchard".

**swerm** – 'n Groot groep insekte of voëls wat saam vlieg. Bv. 'n Swerm bye het in die boom gaan sit. Een swerm, baie swerms. Engels: "swarm".

**bedreig** – Wanneer jy voel iemand of iets wil jou seermaak. Bv. Die kat blaas as hy bedreig voel. Engels: "threatened".

*Extra cards for more support:* gons, byeboer

### Die leesstuk

Wanneer jy 'n by sien, dink jy waarskynlik eerste aan 'n pynlike steek. Baie mense is bang vir bye en probeer hulle so gou moontlik wegjaag. Tog is hierdie klein insekte van die belangrikste diere op ons planeet. Sonder bye sou ons kosborde baie leër en vervelig lyk.

Bye vlieg van blom tot blom op soek na nektar, 'n soet sap waarvan hulle heuning maak. Terwyl hulle drink, kleef fyn, geel stuifmeel aan hul harige lyfies vas. Wanneer die by na die volgende blom vlieg, val van daardie stuifmeel af. Dit word bestuiwing genoem, en sonder dit kan baie plante nie vrugte of saad maak nie. Appels, lemoene, avokado's, pampoene en selfs koffie het almal bestuiwers soos bye nodig. Wetenskaplikes skat dat omtrent een uit elke drie happies kos wat ons eet, op een of ander manier van bestuiwers afhang.

Ongelukkig is bye wêreldwyd in gevaar. Boere en tuiniers gebruik gif om plae dood te maak, maar die gif maak ook bye siek. Wanneer veld en fynbos plek maak vir geboue en paaie, verloor bye hul blomme. Siektes en klein parasiete, soos die varroamyt, verswak ook hele korwe. In party lande moet boere al bye huur om hul boorde te bestuif, omdat daar nie meer genoeg wilde bye is nie.

Die goeie nuus is dat elkeen van ons kan help. Plant inheemse blomme in jou tuin, soos aalwyne, vygies en wilde salie, wat bye baie graag besoek. Laat 'n vlak bakkie water met 'n paar klippies in staan, sodat bye kan drink sonder om te verdrink. Vra jou ouers om nie gif in die tuin te spuit nie, en koop heuning by plaaslike byeboere. As jy 'n swerm bye sien, bly kalm en hou afstand, en vra 'n volwassene om 'n byeverwyderaar te bel eerder as om die bye dood te maak.

Die volgende keer wat 'n by om jou kop gons, onthou hoe hard sy werk. Sy steek gewoonlik net as sy bedreig voel. Eintlik is sy een van die kleinste boere op aarde, wat van dagbreek tot sononder help om ons kos te laat groei. Sonder haar sou ons wêreld baie armer wees.

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Waarvan maak bye heuning?** Van stuifmeel. / **Van nektar. ✓** / Van water. / Van blare. / Van suiker.
2. **Wat is bestuiwing?** Wanneer bye heuning maak. / Wanneer blomme verwelk. / **Wanneer stuifmeel van blom na blom gedra word sodat plante vrugte en saad kan maak. ✓** / Wanneer boere bome plant. / Wanneer bye steek.
3. **Watter twee dinge bedreig bye volgens die leesstuk?** Reën en wind. / Katte en honde. / **Gif en die verlies van blomme. ✓** / Hitte en koue. / Voëls en slange.
4. **(Dinkvraag) Waarom sit jy klippies in die bakkie water?** Om die water koel te hou. / Om die bakkie swaar te maak. / Sodat die water nie verdamp nie. / Om die bye te lok. / **Sodat bye op die klippies kan staan en drink sonder om te verdrink. ✓**
5. **(Dinkvraag) Waarom noem die skrywer die by "een van die kleinste boere op aarde"?** Omdat sy op 'n plaas woon. / Omdat sy 'n trekker bestuur. / **Omdat sy help om ons kos te laat groei. ✓** / Omdat sy heuning verkoop. / Omdat sy groente plant.

### Woordherkenning (10)

pynlike, insekte, nektar, stuifmeel, bestuiwing, wetenskaplikes, parasiete, boorde, inheemse, byeverwyderaar

### Grammatika: die lydende vorm (5)

*Skryf die sin in die lydende vorm – Bv. Die by maak heuning. – Heuning word deur die by gemaak.* (The site should ignore a missing full stop.)

1. Die boer plant bome. – **Bome word deur die boer geplant.**
2. Die kinders pluk appels. – **Appels word deur die kinders gepluk.**
3. Ma koop heuning. – **Heuning word deur Ma gekoop.**
4. Die by besoek die blom. – **Die blom word deur die by besoek.**
5. Pa spuit die tuin. – **Die tuin word deur Pa gespuit.**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. Bye speel 'n groot rol in die \_\_\_\_\_\_ van vrugtebome. bestuur / **bestuiwing ✓** / bestuiwer / bestuiwe
2. Die by se pote is geel van die \_\_\_\_\_\_. **stuifmeel ✓** / stuifsand / stukkie / stuiwer
3. Ons het perskes in die \_\_\_\_\_\_ gepluk. bord / boot / **boord ✓** / boog
4. 'n \_\_\_\_\_\_ bye het in die ou boom gaan sit. Swem / Swerf / Swerms / **Swerm ✓**
5. 'n Slang byt soms as hy \_\_\_\_\_\_ voel. bedrieg / bedryf / bedreiging / **bedreig ✓**

## Level 5 – Afrikaans 3: Kan ons eendag op Mars woon?

Topic: Space and stars. About 342 words, 22 sentences, 5 paragraphs, informative with a question for the reader.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**agentskap** – 'n Organisasie wat 'n spesifieke soort werk doen. Bv. NASA is 'n ruimte-agentskap in Amerika. Een agentskap, baie agentskappe. Engels: "agency".

**dekade** – 'n Tydperk van tien jaar. Bv. My oupa het drie dekades lank op die plaas gewerk. Een dekade, baie dekades. Engels: "decade".

**ondersoek** – Om iets noukeurig te bestudeer om meer daarvan uit te vind. Bv. Die dokter ondersoek my seer oor. Ek ondersoek, ek het ondersoek. Engels: "investigate".

**koolstofdioksied** – 'n Gas wat ons uitasem en wat plante gebruik om kos te maak. Bv. Bome neem koolstofdioksied uit die lug op. Engels: "carbon dioxide".

**oorleef** – Om aan die lewe te bly, veral in 'n moeilike of gevaarlike situasie. Bv. Kamele kan lank sonder water oorleef. Ek oorleef, ek het oorleef. Engels: "survive".

**vervaardig** – Om iets te maak, dikwels in groot hoeveelhede. Bv. Die fabriek vervaardig karre. Ek vervaardig, ek het vervaardig. Engels: "manufacture / produce".

*Extra cards for more support:* kweekhuis, ysteroksied

### Die leesstuk

Mense droom al eeue lank daarvan om na ander planete te reis. Vandag is daardie droom nader as ooit tevore. Ruimte-agentskappe en private maatskappye beplan om binne die volgende paar dekades mense na Mars te stuur. Maar sou jy regtig op die rooi planeet wou woon?

Mars is die vierde planeet van die son af en omtrent die helfte so groot soos die aarde. Dit word die rooi planeet genoem omdat sy grond vol ysteroksied, oftewel roes, is. 'n Dag op Mars is net 'n bietjie langer as op aarde, maar 'n jaar duur amper twee keer so lank. Wetenskaplikes het al robotvoertuie, soos Curiosity en Perseverance, na Mars gestuur om die planeet te ondersoek. Hulle het tekens gevind dat daar lank gelede riviere en mere op Mars was.

Tog sal die lewe op Mars baie moeilik wees. Die lug is so dun dat jy nie kan asemhaal nie, en dit bestaan meestal uit koolstofdioksied. Die gemiddelde temperatuur is ongeveer min sestig grade Celsius, baie kouer as 'n vrieskas. Daar is ook geen beskerming teen die son se gevaarlike strale nie, en groot stofstorms kan maande lank die hele planeet bedek.

Om daar te oorleef, sal mense in spesiale geboue moet woon wat soos groot kweekhuise lyk. Hulle sal hul eie kos moet kweek, water uit ys onder die grond moet haal en suurstof moet vervaardig. Die reis self sal ook omtrent sewe maande duur. Stel jou voor jy moet sewe maande lank in 'n klein ruimtetuig saam met dieselfde paar mense bly!

Party mense dink dit is te duur en te gevaarlik, en dat ons eerder na ons eie planeet moet omsien. Ander glo dat die ondersoek van Mars ons nuwe uitvindings sal gee wat ook die lewe op aarde kan verbeter. Wat dink jy? Sou jy een van die eerste mense op Mars wou wees, of bly jy liewer hier, waar jy vars lug kan inasem en in die see kan swem? Wat ook al jou antwoord is, die vraag laat ons beslis besef hoe spesiaal ons eie planeet is.

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Waarom word Mars die rooi planeet genoem?** Omdat dit baie warm is. / Omdat dit naby die son is. / **Omdat sy grond vol ysteroksied (roes) is. ✓** / Omdat dit rooi wolke het. / Omdat mense dit so geverf het.
2. **Watter tekens het wetenskaplikes op Mars gevind?** Tekens van ou huise. / Tekens van diere. / Tekens van bome. / **Tekens dat daar lank gelede riviere en mere was. ✓** / Tekens van mense.
3. **Waarom sal mense nie op Mars kan asemhaal nie?** **Die lug is te dun en bestaan meestal uit koolstofdioksied. ✓** / Daar is te veel stof. / Dit is te warm. / Die wind is te sterk. / Daar is te veel water.
4. **(Dinkvraag) Waarom sal die sewe maande lange reis moeilik wees?** Omdat daar nie kos is nie. / Omdat die ruimtetuig te vinnig ry. / **Omdat mense lank in 'n klein ruimte met dieselfde paar mense moet bly. ✓** / Omdat dit te warm is. / Omdat daar geen vensters is nie.
5. **(Dinkvraag) Watter punt maak die skrywer in die laaste paragraaf?** Almal moet Mars toe gaan. / Mars is beter as die aarde. / Ruimtereise is maklik. / Ons moet nooit weer die ruimte ondersoek nie. / **Om oor Mars te dink, laat ons besef hoe spesiaal die aarde is. ✓**

### Woordherkenning (10)

planete, agentskappe, dekades, ysteroksied, robotvoertuie, wetenskaplikes, koolstofdioksied, temperatuur, kweekhuise, vervaardig

### Grammatika: sinne verbind met "omdat" (5)

*Verbind die sinne met "omdat" – Bv. Ek bly binne. Dit reën. – Ek bly binne omdat dit reën.* (Watch the word order: the verb moves to the end.)

1. Mars is rooi. Die grond is vol roes. – **Mars is rooi omdat die grond vol roes is.**
2. Ons kan nie daar asemhaal nie. Die lug is te dun. – **Ons kan nie daar asemhaal nie omdat die lug te dun is.**
3. Hulle dra ruimtepakke. Dit is baie koud. – **Hulle dra ruimtepakke omdat dit baie koud is.**
4. Die reis is lank. Mars is baie ver. – **Die reis is lank omdat Mars baie ver is.**
5. Ek wil nie gaan nie. Ek sal die see mis. – **Ek wil nie gaan nie omdat ek die see sal mis.**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. NASA is 'n bekende ruimte-\_\_\_\_\_\_. agent / **agentskap ✓** / agenda / agentskappe
2. My oupa het drie \_\_\_\_\_\_ lank as boer gewerk. dekade / dekens / **dekades ✓** / dekor
3. Kamele kan lank sonder water \_\_\_\_\_\_. **oorleef ✓** / oorlede / oorlaai / oorleg
4. Die fabriek \_\_\_\_\_\_ duisende karre elke jaar. vervaardiging / vervaag / verval / **vervaardig ✓**
5. Bome neem \_\_\_\_\_\_ uit die lug op. koolhidrate / **koolstofdioksied ✓** / kookplaat / koolraap

## Level 5 – Afrikaans 4: Saam is ons sterker

Topic: Sport and games. About 338 words, 23 sentences, 5 paragraphs, persuasive.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**in gemeen** – Wanneer twee of meer mense of dinge dieselfde eienskap het. Bv. Ek en my suster het baie in gemeen. Engels: "in common".

**aktief** – Besig en in beweging; nie stil nie. Bv. Hondjies is baie aktief. Teenoorgestelde: onaktief. Engels: "active".

**vertrou** – Om te glo dat iemand eerlik is en sal doen wat hulle sê. Bv. Ek vertrou my ma om my te help. Ek vertrou, ek het vertrou. Engels: "trust".

**teleurstelling** – Die hartseer gevoel wanneer iets nie gebeur soos jy gehoop het nie. Bv. Die afgelaste uitstappie was 'n groot teleurstelling. Een teleurstelling, baie teleurstellings. Engels: "disappointment".

**veerkragtigheid** – Die vermoë om weer op te staan en aan te gaan ná iets moeiliks. Bv. Haar veerkragtigheid het haar gehelp om ná die besering weer te speel. Engels: "resilience".

**kultuur** – Die gewoontes, tale, kos en feeste van 'n groep mense. Bv. Suid-Afrika het baie verskillende kulture. Een kultuur, baie kulture. Engels: "culture".

*Extra cards for more support:* vleuel, reserwe

### Die leesstuk

Wat het 'n rugbyspeler, 'n netbalspeler en 'n sokkerspeler in gemeen? Hulle weet almal dat niemand 'n wedstryd op sy eie wen nie. Spansport leer ons lesse wat ver buite die speelveld strek. Daarom behoort elke kind die kans te kry om deel van 'n span te wees.

Eerstens hou spansport jou liggaam gesond en sterk. Wanneer jy hardloop, spring en die bal gooi, word jou hart, longe en spiere sterker. Dokters sê kinders behoort elke dag minstens 'n uur lank aktief te wees. Dit is baie makliker om daardie uur vol te maak as jy saam met jou maats oefen en speel.

Tweedens leer 'n span jou om saam te werk. In rugby kan die vinnigste vleuel nie 'n drie druk as die voorspelers nie eers die bal wen nie. In netbal mag die doelskieter nie oral op die baan hardloop nie, en sy moet op haar spanmaats vertrou om die bal na haar toe te bring. Elke speler het 'n rol, en die span slaag net as almal hul deel doen. Dieselfde geld later in die klaskamer, in 'n gesin en by die werk.

Derdens leer spansport jou hoe om te verloor, en dit is dalk die belangrikste les van almal. Niemand wen elke wedstryd nie. Wanneer jou span verloor, leer jy om die ander span se hand te skud, jou foute raak te sien en harder te oefen. Om weer op te staan ná 'n teleurstelling, bou 'n sterkte wat jou jou hele lewe lank sal help. Sielkundiges noem dit veerkragtigheid.

Laastens maak spansport vriende. Die kinders saam met wie jy in die reën oefen en ná 'n wedstryd in die bus sing, word dikwels vriende vir die lewe. Jy leer ook om saam te werk met mense wat anders is as jy, uit ander huise, kulture en tale. Of jy nou die sterspeler is of die reserwe op die bank, jy behoort aan iets groters as jouself. So, waarop wag jy? Kies 'n sport, sluit by 'n span aan, en ontdek hoe sterk jy saam met ander kan wees.

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Hoe lank moet kinders volgens dokters elke dag aktief wees?** Tien minute. / 'n Halfuur. / **Minstens 'n uur. ✓** / Drie uur. / Die hele dag.
2. **Waarom kan die vinnigste vleuel nie op sy eie 'n drie druk nie?** Hy is te moeg. / Hy mag nie hardloop nie. / **Die voorspelers moet eers die bal wen. ✓** / Die afrigter laat hom nie toe nie. / Die bal is te swaar.
3. **Wat noem sielkundiges die vermoë om ná 'n teleurstelling weer op te staan?** Spanwerk. / Vriendskap. / Fiksheid. / **Veerkragtigheid. ✓** / Kultuur.
4. **(Dinkvraag) Waarom sê die skrywer dat om te verloor dalk die belangrikste les is?** Omdat verloor lekker is. / **Omdat dit jou leer om aan te gaan en beter te word, wat jou hele lewe help. ✓** / Omdat almal altyd verloor. / Omdat wen nie belangrik is nie. / Omdat die ander span dan bly is.
5. **(Dinkvraag) Wat wil die skrywer hê moet die leser doen?** Net na sport op TV kyk. / Alleen oefen. / Net rugby speel. / Ophou om sport te speel. / **By 'n span aansluit en saam met ander sport speel. ✓**

### Woordherkenning (10)

rugbyspeler, speelveld, aktief, voorspelers, doelskieter, spanmaats, teleurstelling, sielkundiges, veerkragtigheid, kulture

### Grammatika: meervoude (5)

*Gee die meervoud – Bv. hond – honde*

1. vleuel – **vleuels**
2. spanmaat – **spanmaats**
3. kultuur – **kulture**
4. long – **longe**
5. spier – **spiere**

### Woordeskat (5)

1. Ek en my neef het baie \_\_\_\_\_\_; ons hou albei van krieket. gemeen / **in gemeen ✓** / algemeen / gemeente
2. Kinders moet elke dag \_\_\_\_\_\_ wees. aktiwiteit / akteur / **aktief ✓** / aksie
3. Ek \_\_\_\_\_\_ my ma om my altyd te help. **vertrou ✓** / vertroue / vertrek / vertroos
4. Die afgelaste uitstappie was 'n groot \_\_\_\_\_\_. teleurgesteld / telefoon / teleskoop / **teleurstelling ✓**
5. Suid-Afrika het baie verskillende \_\_\_\_\_\_. kultuur / **kulture ✓** / kulturele / kultivar

## Level 5 – Afrikaans 5: Kos is kosbaar

Topic: Food and cooking. About 337 words, 22 sentences, 5 paragraphs, informative and persuasive.

### Woordkaarte (6)

**verlep** – Wanneer plante of groente slap en droog word omdat hulle te oud is of water kort. Bv. Die blomme het in die vaas verlep. Dit verlep, dit het verlep. Engels: "wilt".

**navorser** – Iemand wat dinge noukeurig bestudeer om nuwe kennis te vind. Bv. Die navorser het die voëls 'n jaar lank dopgehou. Een navorser, baie navorsers. Engels: "researcher".

**produseer** – Om iets te maak of te kweek. Bv. Die plaas produseer melk en kaas. Ek produseer, ek het geproduseer. Engels: "produce".

**arbeid** – Harde werk, veral fisiese werk. Bv. Om 'n huis te bou, verg baie arbeid. Engels: "labour".

**etiket** – 'n Stukkie papier of plastiek op 'n produk met inligting daaroor. Bv. Lees die etiket om te sien wat in die koekies is. Een etiket, baie etikette. Engels: "label".

**liefdadigheidsorganisasie** – 'n Organisasie wat mense in nood help sonder om wins te maak. Bv. Die liefdadigheidsorganisasie deel komberse in die winter uit. Engels: "charity".

*Extra cards for more support:* oorskietkos, inkopielys

### Die leesstuk

Dink aan die laaste keer toe jy kos in die asblik gegooi het. Miskien was dit 'n halwe toebroodjie, 'n brood wat te lank gestaan het, of groente wat in die yskas verlep het. Dit lyk dalk na 'n klein ding, maar al daardie klein bietjies tel vinnig op.

Navorsers skat dat omtrent 'n derde van al die kos wat in Suid-Afrika geproduseer word, nooit geëet word nie. Dit is soos om drie sakke kos by die winkel te koop en een sommer op pad huis toe in die asblik te gooi. Dit is ongeveer tien miljoen ton kos elke jaar. Terselfdertyd gaan miljoene mense in ons land gereeld honger slaap. Kos word op elke stap verspil: op plase, in fabrieke, in winkels en by die huis.

Wanneer ons kos weggooi, mors ons nie net die kos self nie. Ons mors ook al die water, grond, brandstof en arbeid wat nodig was om dit te kweek en te vervoer. Boonop vrot die kos op stortingsterreine, waar dit gasse vrystel wat help om die aarde warmer te maak.

Gelukkig kan elke gesin help. Beplan jou maaltye vir die week en skryf 'n inkopielys, sodat jy net koop wat jy nodig het. Sit nuwe kos agter in die yskas en ouer kos voor, sodat dit eerste gebruik word. Verstaan die verskil tussen "gebruik voor" en "beste voor" op etikette: kos wat verby sy "beste voor"-datum is, is dikwels nog heeltemal veilig om te eet. Maak oorskietkos in nuwe geregte om; gisteraand se rys kan vandag se rysslaai word. Skille en blare kan op 'n komposhoop gaan, waar dit kos vir die tuin word.

Party skole en winkels skenk ook kos wat nog goed is aan liefdadigheidsorganisasies, wat dit aan mense in nood gee. Jou klas kan dalk so 'n projek begin. Onthou: elke happie wat jy spaar, is 'n happie wat iemand anders kan voed of wat die aarde nie hoef te dra nie. Kos is kosbaar. Behandel dit so, en jy sal verbaas wees hoeveel verskil een gesin kan maak.

### Begrip (5 vrae, ✓ = reg)

1. **Hoeveel van die kos wat in Suid-Afrika geproduseer word, word nooit geëet nie?** Byna niks. / 'n Tiende. / **Omtrent 'n derde. ✓** / Die helfte. / Alles.
2. **Wat mors ons behalwe die kos self as ons kos weggooi?** Net die verpakking. / Ons tyd. / **Die water, grond, brandstof en arbeid wat nodig was om dit te kweek en te vervoer. ✓** / Ons geld alleen. / Niks anders nie.
3. **Wat sê die leesstuk oor kos wat verby sy "beste voor"-datum is?** Dit is altyd giftig. / Dit moet dadelik weggegooi word. / Dit smaak altyd sleg. / **Dit is dikwels nog heeltemal veilig om te eet. ✓** / Dit mag nie verkoop word nie.
